occurs for example in the rules

Measure*
,
MeasureForget*
, and
Sample*

for measurements and random sampling.
We discuss the predicate
\mathbf{class}({\mathbf{X}})

and its uses in greater depth in

Section 7.2
.

6
Core Rules

In this section, we present the core reasoning rules for our
logic. Since we have defined the logic semantically
(
Definition 2
), the set of rules is not fixed a priori (since
we can always prove additional rules sound). Nevertheless, we identify
a set of important rules (one per language primitive, plus some useful
structural rules) that form the basis of the rest of this paper. In
particular, all “derived rules” in
Section 7
are a consequence
of these core rules. That is, after this section we can “forget”

Definition 2
and build only on the rules from this
section. (Convenient additional rules will be derived in later
sections as corollaries.)

6.1
Rules for individual statements

For each command of our language (sequence, skip, initialization,
application, if, while), we introduce one rule that derives a Hoare
judgment for that command from judgments about its subterms. The
rules for sequence and skip are quite obvious and follow directly from
the definition:

\frac{\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}\hskip 8.19447pt\{{\mathsf{B}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}\,}\{{\mathsf{C}}\}}{\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}};\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}\,}\{{\mathsf{C}}\}}

Seq

\frac{\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{B}}{\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\underline{\mathbf{skip}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}}

Skip

More interesting are the rules for operations on quantum states (isometries, initialization):

\frac{}{\bigl\{{\mathsf{A}}\bigr\}{\,{\underline{\mathbf{apply}}}\ U\ \underline{\mathbf{to}}\ \mathbf{X}\,}\bigl\{{(U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X})\cdot\mathsf{A}}\bigr\}}

Apply

\frac{}{\bigl\{{\mathsf{A}}\bigr\}{\,\underline{\mathbf{init}}\ {\mathbf{x}}\,}\bigl\{{\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{e}/{\mathbf{x}}}\},\ {{\mathbf{x}}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\lvert{0}\rangle}}\bigr\}}

Init

Apply
says that applying an isometry
U

to variables
\mathbf{X}

has the effect of multiplying the predicate
\mathsf{A}

with
U

(after suitably lifting
U

to operate on quantum memories, see
\autopageref
page:opon
for the definition of
U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}
).
Init
is more interesting
because it is the first rule that introduces ghosts. Since
initialization “overwrites” the original value of
{\mathbf{x}}
,

{\mathbf{x}}

becomes an engangled ghost, thus the precondition
\mathsf{A}

is replaced by
\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{e}/{\mathbf{x}}}\}
,
i.e.,
{\mathbf{x}}

is replaced by a fresh ghost
\mathbf{e}
.
(
\mathbf{e}
is fresh, i.e.,
\notin\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{A})
, because otherwise
\AA\{\mathbf{e}/{\mathbf{x}}\}
would
not be welltyped.)
Additionally,
{\mathbf{x}}

will afterwards be in the state
\lvert{0}\rangle
,
so the postcondition additionally contains
{\mathbf{x}}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\lvert{0}\rangle
.
The rules
Apply
and
Init
are shown in

lemmas

10
and
11
in
Section 6.3
.

The rules for if and while do not introduce ghosts
and are the same as in “regular” quantum Hoare logic:

\frac{\bigl\{{\bigl({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{1}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}}\bigr)\cdot\mathsf{A}}\bigr\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\bigl\{{\mathsf{B}}\bigr\}\hskip 8.58807pt\bigl\{{\bigl({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}}\bigr)\cdot\mathsf{A}}\bigr\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}\,}\bigl\{{\mathsf{B}}\bigr\}}{\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\underline{\mathbf{if}}\ {\mathbf{x}}\ \underline{\mathbf{then}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\ \underline{\mathbf{else}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}}

If

\frac{\bigl\{{\bigl({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{1}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}}\bigr)\cdot\mathsf{A}}\bigr\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\bigl\{{\mathsf{A}}\bigr\}}{\bigl\{{\mathsf{A}}\bigr\}{\,\underline{\mathbf{while}}\ {\mathbf{x}}\ \underline{\mathbf{do}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\bigl\{{\bigl({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}}\bigr)\cdot\mathsf{A}}\bigr\}}

While

Since the if-statement measures
{\mathbf{x}}

before executing
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}

or
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}

(see
Section 3
), the precondition
\mathsf{A}

becomes
\bigl({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{1}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}}\bigr)\cdot\mathsf{A}

when that measurement returns
1

and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}

is executed (as
\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{1}\rangle})

is the projector corresponding to measurement outcome
1
),
and it becomes
\bigl({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}}\bigr)\cdot\mathsf{A}

if the measurement returns
0

and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}

is executed. Thus analyzing
\underline{\mathbf{if}}\ {\mathbf{x}}\ \underline{\mathbf{then}}\ \mathsf{A}\ \underline{\mathbf{else}}\ \mathsf{B}

reduces to analyzing
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}

and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}
with those two respective preconditions.

Similarly,
\underline{\mathbf{while}}\ {\mathbf{x}}\ \underline{\mathbf{do}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}

executes
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}

after measuring
{\mathbf{x}}

and getting
1
. Thus, if we use
\mathsf{A}

as the loop invariant, the postcondition for the loop body becomes

({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{1}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\cdot\mathsf{A}
.
And to end the loop, the measurement of
{\mathbf{x}}

must return
0
,
hence we get the postcondition
({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\cdot\mathsf{A}

for the overall loop.
The rules
If
and
While
are proven in

lemmas

12
and
13
in
Section 6.3
.

6.2
Further core rules

Besides the per-statement rules from the previous section, we will use
five more rules, related to case-distinctions and to the modification
of ghosts. First, we consider case-distinctions. In classical Hoare
logic, we can easily show the following rule:

\forall z.\,\{{{\mathbf{x}}=z,\ \mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}\implies\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}
.
That is, to show
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}
,
it is sufficient to consider each possible value
z

of
{\mathbf{x}}

separately and prove
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}

under the additional assumption that
{\mathbf{x}}=z

holds in the precondition. An immediate quantum analogue would be:

\forall\psi.\,\{{{\mathbf{x}}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}\implies\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}
. There are two problems with such this rule. First, it does
not hold in this generality:
{\mathbf{x}}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi

implies that
{\mathbf{x}}

is not entangled with any other variables (because it is in the
specific pure state
\psi
),
so proving
\{{{\mathbf{x}}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}

for all
\psi

does not guarantee anything about the behavior of
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}

in the presence of entanglement.
18
18

18

Formally, a counterexample
would be:
\mathsf{A}:=({\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{y}}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\lvert{00}\rangle+\lvert{11}\rangle)
,

\mathsf{B}:=\bot
,
and
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}:=\underline{\mathbf{skip}}
.
Then for all
\psi
,

({\mathbf{x}}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A})=\bot
,
hence
\{{{\mathbf{x}}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}}\}{\,\underline{\mathbf{skip}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}
.
But
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}

does not hold.

And even if we fix this by adding suitable extra
conditions, the rule will force us to always quantify over all
possible
\psi
.
But if, for example,
{\mathbf{x}}

is guaranteed to be classical (e.g.,
\mathsf{A}=\mathbf{class}({{\mathbf{x}}})
)
then we would like to only consider the cases
{\mathbf{x}}=\lvert{z}\rangle
.
To formulate a rule that solves both problems, we introduce an
additional concept:

Definition 5
(Disentangling)

A predicate
\mathsf{A}
on
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{U}
is

M
-disentangling

(for a
set

M\subseteq\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}}]
) iff:
For all sets of variables
\mathbf{V}
(disjoint from
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{U}
),
all quantum memories
\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}\neq 0
over
\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}
,
and all quantum memories
\psi_{\mathbf{U}}\neq 0
over
\mathbf{U}

with
\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U}}\in\mathsf{A}
,
we have that
\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}=\psi_{\mathbf{V}}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{X}}

for some
\psi_{\mathbf{V}}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{V}}]
and some
\psi_{\mathbf{X}}\in M
.

What does this definition mean? Roughly speaking, it means that if
variables
\mathbf{X}

and
\mathbf{U}
,
jointly, satisfy
\mathsf{A}
,
and variables
\mathbf{U}

are not entangled with variables
\mathbf{X}

or
\mathbf{V}
,
then we know that variables
\mathbf{X}

are also not entangled with variables
\mathbf{V}
,
and additionally that variables
\mathbf{X}
will be in one of the states in
M
.

A trivial example would be
\mathsf{A}:=({\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{u}=\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2})

which is
\{\phi_{1}\}
-disentangling.
That is, if
{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{u}

are in state
\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2}
,
then
{\mathbf{x}}

is in state
\phi_{1}

(unsurprisingly). Similarly, for any non-separable
\phi
,

S=\operatorname{span}\{\phi\}

is
\varnothing
-disentangling
(as the variables
{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{u}
cannot at the same time be non-entangled and in state
\phi
).
The following lemma gives two more interesting examples of disentangling predicates:

Lemma 6

Let
T

be the type of
\mathbf{X}
.
Then
\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{q}}\mathbf{U}
and
\mathbf{separable}({\mathbf{X}})

are
\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}}]
-disentangling.
And
\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{c\mkern-1.0mul}}\mathbf{U}
and
\mathbf{class}({\mathbf{X}})
are
\{\lvert{i}\rangle\}_{i\in T}
-disentangling.

Proof.
We first show that
\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{q}}\mathbf{U}

is
\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}}]
-disentangling.
(This also implies that
\mathbf{separable}({\mathbf{X}})

is
\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}}]
-disentangling
since
\mathbf{separable}({\mathbf{X}})

is simply syntactic sugar for
\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{q}}\mathbf{u}
.)
Fix some variables
\mathbf{V}
,
and quantum memories
\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}

and
\psi_{\mathbf{U}}

over
\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}

and
\mathbf{U}
,
respectively, with

\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U}}\in(\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{q}}\mathbf{U})
.
By
Lemma 3
(with
\mathbf{V}:=\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}
,

\mathbf{W}:=\mathbf{X}
,

\mathbf{V}^{\prime}:=\mathbf{W}^{\prime}:=\mathbf{U}
),
this implies that
\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}

can be written as
\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}=\psi_{\mathbf{V}}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{X}}

for some quantum memories
\psi_{\mathbf{V}},\psi_{\mathbf{X}}

over
\mathbf{V},\mathbf{X}
,
respectively. And trivially,
\psi_{\mathbf{X}}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}}]
. Thus

\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{q}}\mathbf{U}

is
\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}}]
-disentangling by
Definition 5
.

Now we show that
\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{c\mkern-1.0mul}}\mathbf{U}

is
\{\lvert{i}\rangle\}_{i\in T}
-disentangling.
(This also implies that
\mathbf{class}({\mathbf{X}})

is
\{\lvert{i}\rangle\}_{i\in T}
-disentangling
since
\mathbf{class}({\mathbf{X}})

is simply syntactic sugar for
\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{c\mkern-1.0mul}}\mathbf{u}
.)
Fix some variables
\mathbf{V}
,
and quantum memories
\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}

and
\psi_{\mathbf{U}}

over
\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}

and
\mathbf{U}
,
respectively, with

\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U}}\in(\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{c\mkern-1.0mul}}\mathbf{U})
and
\psi_{\mathbf{U}}\neq 0
.
By
Definition 5
, we need to show that

\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}=\psi_{\mathbf{V}}\otimes\lvert{i}\rangle_{\mathbf{X}}

for some
i\in T

and
\psi_{\mathbf{V}}

over
\mathbf{V}
.
We decompose

\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}=\sum\lambda_{i}\psi_{\mathbf{V},i}\otimes\lvert{i}\rangle_{\mathbf{X}}

and
\psi_{\mathbf{U}}=\sum\lambda_{i}^{\prime}\lvert{i}\rangle_{\mathbf{U}}
for some
\psi_{\mathbf{V},i}\neq 0
over
\mathbf{V}
.
Since
\psi_{\mathbf{U}}\neq 0
,
there exists a
j

such that
\lambda^{\prime}_{j}\neq 0
.
If
\lambda_{i}\neq 0

for some
i\neq j
,
then
\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U}}

is not in the span of states of the form

\lvert{\nu}\rangle_{\mathbf{X}}\otimes\lvert{\nu}\rangle_{\mathbf{U}}\otimes\dots
,
in contradiction to

\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U}}\in(\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{c\mkern-1.0mul}}\mathbf{U})
.
Thus
\lambda_{i}=0

for all
i\neq j
, hence
\psi_{\mathbf{V}\mathbf{X}}=\lambda_{j}\psi_{\mathbf{V},j}\otimes\lvert{j}\rangle_{\mathbf{X}}
as desired. Thus

\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{c\mkern-1.0mul}}\mathbf{U}

is
\{\lvert{i}\rangle\}_{i\in T}
-disentangling.

\Box

Armed with the definition of disentangling predicates, we can formulate the rule for case
distinctions:

\frac{\text{$\mathsf{C}$ is $M$-disentangling predicate on $\mathbf{X}\mathbf{U}$}\quad\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{C}\quad\forall\psi\in M.\ \{{\mathbf{X}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}}{\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}}\quad\textsc{{Case}{\@index{\@indexphrase[Case]{{Case} (rule)}}}\addcontentsline{rule}{rule}{{Case}}
}

As a special case (with
\mathsf{C}:=\mathbf{class}({{\mathbf{x}}})

and using
Lemma 6
), we can recover a rule for
case distinction over classical variables:

\forall z.\{{\mathbf{X}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\lvert{z}\rangle,\ \mathbf{class}({\mathbf{X}}),\ \mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}\implies\{{\mathbf{class}({\mathbf{X}}),\ \mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}
. See the derived
rule
CaseClassical
on

\autopageref
rule:CaseClassical for details. Notice that we would
not have been able to even state such a case rule without using ghosts!
The
Case
rule is proven in
Lemma 14
in

Section 6.3
.

The
Case
rule has the disadvantage that we need to have a
disentangling predicate in the precondition. As described above, this
is necessary because the variable under consideration might be
entangled with other variables. However, if we make a case distinction
over the state of
all
variables, then this requirement
disappears. In fact, it turns out that it is enough to make a case
distinction over the state of the free variables in program and
pre-/postconditions plus one extra variable
{\mathbf{x}}
(this is not obvious because those variables might
still be entangled with other variables that are not used but
nevertheless present, even variables with uncountable type):

\frac{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}\supseteq\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{A},\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}})\quad\mathbf{X}\supseteq\mathsf{progvars}(\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{B}))\quad{\mathbf{x}}\notin\mathbf{X}\quad\text{type of ${\mathbf{x}}$ is infinite}\quad\quad\forall\psi\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{E}}],\psi^{\prime}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{U}}],\ \psi,\psi^{\prime}\neq 0.\ \{{\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathbf{U}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi^{\prime},\ \mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}}{\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}}\quad\textsc{{Universe}{\@index{\@indexphrase[Universe]{{Universe} (rule)}}}\addcontentsline{rule}{rule}{{Universe}}
}

(We call this rule
Universe
since we do a case distinction
over the state of all variables, i.e., of the whole universe.) We
will see an example where the
Universe
rule is useful in
the analysis of the quantum one-time pad (
Section 8.3
, general case).
Note that it is important in this rule that we can fix one
concrete set
\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}

of variables to quantify over. Otherwise, we would have to quantify
over all states
over all possible sets of variables
; depending
on the precise formalization the “set” of all possible sets of
variables might not even be a set, and a rigorous formalization of the
rule may not be possible in logical foundations that do not allow us
to quantify over large classes (e.g., higher-order logic as formalized
in Isabelle/HOL
[
17
]
). The rule is proven in

Lemma 16
in
Section 6.3
.

For stating the next rules more readably, we introduce another
notation: We write
\mathsf{A}\Rrightarrow\mathsf{B}

for
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\underline{\mathbf{skip}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}

(which in turn is equivalent to

\forall\rho.\ \rho\vDash\mathsf{A}\implies\rho\vDash\mathsf{B}
).
By rules
Seq
and
Skip
(and the fact that
\underline{\mathbf{skip}}

is the neutral element of
;
)
we immediately have that
\Rrightarrow

is a preorder that refines
\subseteq
.
Also note that rule
Seq
implies that
\mathsf{A}\Rrightarrow\mathsf{A}^{\prime},\ \{{\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}^{\prime}}\},\ \mathsf{B}^{\prime}\Rrightarrow\mathsf{B}\implies\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}
,
so
\Rrightarrow
can be used for rewriting Hoare judgments.

The next three rules are specific to ghost variables and allow us to
rewrite predicates.

\frac{}{A\Rrightarrow\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}/\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U}^{\prime}/\mathbf{U}}\}}

Rename

\frac{\forall i.\operatorname{rank}M_{i}\leq 1\hskip 8.19447pt\sum\nolimits_{i}M_{i}^{*}{M_{i}}=\mathsf{id}\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\text{$\mathbf{G}$ either all entangled or all unentangled ghosts}\hskip 8.19447pt\text{$\mathbf{G}^{\prime}$ either all entangled or all unentangled ghosts}}{\mathsf{A}\Rrightarrow\bigvee\nolimits_{\!i}\Bigl({({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})\cdot\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{G}^{\prime}/\mathbf{G}}\}}\Bigr)}

Transmute

\frac{\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi})=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi^{\prime}})\hskip 8.19447pt(\mathbf{E}\cup\mathbf{E}^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{fv}(A)=\varnothing}{({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}})\Rrightarrow({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi^{\prime},\ \mathsf{A}})}

ShapeShift

Rule
Rename
simply allows us to rename ghosts, this mainly allows us to tidy up
judgments.
Rule
Rename
follows directly from
definitions

1
and
2
.
When reading
Transmute
, recall that
\mathbf{G},\mathbf{G}^{\prime}

may refer to both entangled and unentangled
ghosts. The purpose of the
Transmute
rule is to change an
entangled ghost into an unentangled ghost or vice versa. (That is, we
will usually have
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{e}

and
\mathbf{G}^{\prime}=\mathbf{u}

or vice versa.) Ideally, we would like to have something like

\mathsf{A}\Rrightarrow\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{u}/\mathbf{e}}\}=:\mathsf{A}^{\prime}

and vice versa, i.e., being able to change the kinds of ghost
variables freely. But of course, that would mean that entangled and
unentangled ghosts are equivalent, and we would not have to had to
distinguish between those different kinds of variables in the first
place. Instead, we get a somewhat more complicate rule where, after
replacing
\mathbf{u}

by
\mathbf{e}

or vice versa, we also need to replace
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}

by

\bigvee\nolimits_{\!i}\bigl({({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})\cdot\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\bigr)
. (Recall that
\vee

is the disjunction of predicates, i.e., the sum of subspaces,
see
\autopageref
page:vee. Hence
\bigvee_{\!i}

is a disjunction of a family of predicates

({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})\cdot\mathsf{A}^{\prime}
.)
The rule will be most useful if we can chose the
M_{i}

in such a way that

\bigvee\nolimits_{\!i}\bigl({({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})\cdot\mathsf{A}^{\prime}}\bigr)=\mathsf{A}^{\prime}
. We will see later that this is often possible
when classical variables are involved (i.e., when the precondition
contains
\mathbf{class}({\mathbf{X}})
).

An example of using
rule
Transmute
analyzes the predicate
\mathbf{distrib}({\mathbf{X},D})
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{distrib}({\mathbf{X},D})

\displaystyle=({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{e}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi_{DD}})\Rrightarrow{\textstyle\bigvee_{i}({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{i}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{u}})\cdot({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{u}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi_{DD}})}

\displaystyle={\textstyle\bigvee_{i}\bigl({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{u}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\sqrt{D(i)}\,\lvert{i}\rangle\otimes\lvert{i}\rangle}\bigr)}\subseteq\begin{cases}({\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{q}}\mathbf{u}})=\mathbf{separable}({\mathbf{X}})\\
({\mathbf{X}\equiv_{\mathfrak{c\mkern-1.0mul}}\mathbf{u}})=\mathbf{class}({\mathbf{X}}).\end{cases}

Here
rule
Transmute
is applied with
M_{i}:=\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{i}\rangle})
,
\mathbf{G}:=\mathbf{e}
,
\mathbf{G}^{\prime}:=\mathbf{u}
.
Thus
\mathbf{distrib}({\mathbf{X},D})
implies that
\mathbf{X}
is separable and classical.

The main purpose of
ShapeShift
, in contrast, is to rewrite
the state
\psi

in predicates of the form
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi
.
The
ShapeShift
rule
has the precondition

\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi})=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi^{\prime}})
.
That is, after tracing out (erasing)
\mathbf{E},\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
,
the two states
\psi,\psi^{\prime}

(interpreted as density operators by applying
\mathsf{proj}({\cdot})
)
should be identical. Or, stated differently, looking only at
\mathbf{X}
,

\psi

and
\psi^{\prime}

have to look identical. Thus the rule says, roughly, that in a predicate

\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi
,
we can replace
\psi

by any state that looks identical from the point of view of
\mathbf{X}
.
For example,
{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{e}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\lvert{00}\rangle+\lvert{11}\rangle\ \Rrightarrow\ {\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{e}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\lvert{01}\rangle+\lvert{10}\rangle
.

Both
Transmute
and
ShapeShift
are be
extensively used in the derivations of derived rules in

Section 7.1
.
We refer to those derivations for examples as to how and where

Transmute
and
ShapeShift
can be used.

ShapeShift
is also used as the core step in the security
proof of the quantum one-time pad (
Section 8.3
).
The rules are proven in
lemmas

17
and
18
,
respectively.

6.3
Proofs of core rules

We begin with some auxiliary lemmas:

Lemma 7

Let
\rho

be a mixed memory over
\mathbf{V}\mathbf{W}

and
\mathsf{A}

a predicate over
\mathbf{V}
.
Then
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho\subseteq{{\mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}(\mathbf{W})}}

iff
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}{\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{W}}\rho}\subseteq\mathsf{A}
.

Proof.
For a predicate
\mathsf{B}
,
let
P_{\mathsf{B}}

denote the projector onto
\mathsf{B}
.
Then for a mixed memory
\sigma=\sum_{i}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{i}})
,

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\sigma\subseteq\mathsf{B}

iff
\forall i.\ \psi_{i}\in\mathsf{B}

iff
\forall i.\ \lVert{P_{\mathsf{B}}\psi_{i}}\rVert=\lVert{\psi_{i}}\rVert

iff

\forall i.\operatorname{tr}P_{\mathsf{B}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{i}})P_{\mathsf{B}}^{*}=\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{i}})

iff
\operatorname{tr}P_{\mathsf{B}}\sigma P_{\mathsf{B}}^{*}=\operatorname{tr}\sigma
. (The last step uses that

\operatorname{tr}P_{\mathsf{B}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{i}})P_{\mathsf{B}}^{*}\leq\operatorname{tr}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{i}})
for all
i
.)

We have

\operatorname{tr}P_{\mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{W}}]}\rho P_{\mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{W}}]}^{*}=\operatorname{tr}({P_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{W}}})\rho({P_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{W}}})^{*}\\
=\operatorname{tr}\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{W}}({P_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{W}}})\rho({P_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{W}}})^{*}=\operatorname{tr}{P_{\mathsf{A}}}({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{W}}\rho}){P_{\mathsf{A}}}^{*}

Thus
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho\subseteq{{\mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}(\mathbf{W})}}

iff
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{W}}\rho\subseteq\mathsf{A}
.

\Box

Lemma 8

If
\mathsf{A}
is a predicate,
and
\rho_{i}

are a family of mixed memories with
\rho_{i}\vDash\mathsf{A}

for all
i
, and
\sum_{i}\rho_{i}
exists,
then
\sum_{i}\rho_{i}\vDash\mathsf{A}
.

Proof.
\mathsf{A}
is a predicate over
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}
, and
\rho_{i}
are mixed memories over
\mathbf{X}
for some
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}
.
Since
\rho_{i}\vDash\mathsf{A}
, there are
(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable
\rho^{\circ}_{i}

with
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho_{i}^{\circ}\subseteq\mathsf{A}

and
\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}_{i}=\rho_{i}
.
We have

\sum\operatorname{tr}\rho_{i}^{\circ}=\sum\operatorname{tr}\rho_{i}<\infty
, since
\sum\rho_{i}
exists.
Thus

\hat{\rho}^{\circ}:=\sum\rho_{i}^{\circ}

exists.

Then

\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\hat{\rho}^{\circ}=\sum\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\rho_{i}^{\circ}=\sum\rho_{i}
. And since all
\rho^{\circ}_{i}

are
(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable,
so is
\hat{\rho}^{\circ}
. Finally,
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\hat{\rho}^{\circ}=\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\sum\rho_{i}^{\circ}=\sum\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho_{i}^{\circ}\subseteq\mathsf{A}
.
Thus
\sum\rho_{i}\vDash\mathsf{A}
, as desired.

\Box

Lemma 9

Let
\rho

be a mixed memory over
\mathbf{X}
, let
\mathsf{A}
be a predicate, and assume
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
.

(i)

Let
M

be an operator from
\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}}]

to
\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}^{\prime}}]
.
Then
M\rho M^{*}\vDash({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\mathsf{A}
.

(ii)

Let
\mathbf{Y}\subseteq\mathbf{X}
and
\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{A})\cap\mathbf{Y}=\varnothing

and
N

be an operator from from
\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{Y}}]

to
\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}}]
.
Then
({N\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}})\rho({N\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}})^{*}\vDash\mathsf{A}
.

Proof.
We first prove (
i
).
Let
\mathcal{E}(\sigma):=M\sigma M^{*}

for all
\sigma
.
Then
\rho^{\prime}:=\mathcal{E}(\rho)

is a mixed memory over
\mathbf{X}^{\prime}
,
and we need to show
\rho^{\prime}\vDash({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\mathsf{A}
.
And in the judgment
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
,
\mathsf{A}

is a predicate over some variables
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}
for some variables
\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}
,
and in the judgment
\rho^{\prime}\vDash M\cdot\mathsf{A}
,
it is interpreted as a predicate over variables
\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}

(see
\autopageref
page:pred.identify).

Since
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
,
there exists a
(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable

\rho^{\circ}

with
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq\mathsf{A}

and
\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}=\rho
.
Let

\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}:=(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}})(\rho^{\circ})
.
Then
\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\prime}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable.
Furthermore,

\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}=(\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}}\otimes\operatorname{tr})\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}})(\rho^{\circ})=(\mathcal{E}\otimes\operatorname{tr})(\rho^{\circ})=\mathcal{E}(\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ})=\mathcal{E}(\rho)=\rho^{\prime}.

(Here
\operatorname{tr}

is seen as a superoperator from the trace-class operators over
\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}

to the trace-class operators on the
1
-dimensional space
\mathbb{C}
.)

And finally, we can write
\rho^{\circ}=\sum_{i}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{i}})

for some
\psi_{i}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}]
and thus

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\,(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}})(\rho^{\circ})=\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\,({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\rho^{\circ}({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})^{*}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\sum\nolimits_{i}\mathsf{proj}\bigl({({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\psi_{i}}\bigr)=\operatorname{span}\{{({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\psi_{i}}\}_{i}=({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\operatorname{span}\{{\psi_{i}}\}_{i}

\displaystyle=({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\mathsf{A}

Thus
\rho^{\prime}\vDash({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\mathsf{A}
.
This shows (
i
).

We now show (
ii
) by reduction to
(
i
). Let
M:=({N\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}})
. Then by (
i
),

{({N\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}})\rho({N\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}})^{*}}=M\rho M^{*}\vDash({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\mathsf{A}
. Thus we need to show that

{({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\mathsf{A}}\subseteq\mathsf{A}
.

Fix
\psi\in{({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\mathsf{A}}
.
Then there exists
\psi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{A}

with
\psi=({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\psi^{\prime}
.

\mathsf{A}
is a predicate on
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}
for some
\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}
.
Let
P
be the projector onto
\mathsf{A}
. Since
\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{A})\cap\mathbf{Y}=\varnothing
,
we can write
P=\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}}\otimes P^{\prime}
for some projector
P^{\prime}
on
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{Y}
.
And when
\mathsf{A}
is interpreted as a predicate on
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{Y}\cup\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}

(as, e.g., in the judgment
({N\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}})\rho({N\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}})^{*}\vDash\mathsf{A}
),
then the projector onto
\mathsf{A}
is
\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}}\otimes P^{\prime}
.
We have

\displaystyle(\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}}\otimes P^{\prime})\psi

\displaystyle=P(N\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{Y}}])\psi^{\prime}=({\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}}\otimes P^{\prime}})(N\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{Y}}])\psi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=(N\otimes P^{\prime})\psi^{\prime}=({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})({\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}}\otimes P^{\prime}})\psi^{\prime}={({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})}P\psi^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}{({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})}\psi^{\prime}=\psi.

Here
(*)

follows since
\psi^{\prime}

is in
\mathsf{A}
,
the image of the projector
P
.
Thus
({\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}}\otimes P^{\prime}})\psi=\psi
,
hence
\psi
is in the image of
\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}^{\prime}}\otimes P
, hence
\psi\in\mathsf{A}
.

Since this holds for all
\psi\in{({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\mathsf{A}}
, this implies
{({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\mathsf{A}}\subseteq\mathsf{A}
.
With

({N\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}})\rho({N\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}})^{*}\vDash({M\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})\cdot\mathsf{A}
, we get

({N\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}})\rho({N\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}})^{*}\vDash\mathsf{A}
. This shows (
ii
).

\Box

Lemma 10

Rule
Apply
is sound.

Proof.
The predicate
\mathsf{A}

is a space of quantum memories over some variables
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}

with
\mathbf{X}\subseteq\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}
.
In this proof, we will encounter both the term
U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}

interpreted as an operator on quantum memories over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}
,
and over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}
.
Since the syntax
U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}

does not disambiguate between the two (the space we are operating on
is left implicit), we write
({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}}
and
({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}
, respectively.
Note that
({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}=({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}
.

We need to show that for any mixed memory
\rho

over
\mathbf{X}
,

\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}

implies
\llbracket{{\underline{\mathbf{apply}}}\ U\ \underline{\mathbf{to}}\ \mathbf{X}}\rrbracket(\rho)\vDash({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\cdot\mathsf{A}
.
Since
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
, there exists an
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable
\rho^{\circ}

with
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq\mathsf{A}

and
\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}=\rho
.
Since
\rho^{\circ}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable,
we can write it as

\rho^{\circ}=\sum_{i}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},i}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U},i}})
.
Since
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq\mathsf{A}
, this implies that

\psi_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},i}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U},i}\in\mathsf{A}
for all
i
.

Let

\hat{\rho}^{\circ}:=\sum_{i}\mathsf{proj}\Bigl({\bigl({({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}}\ \psi_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},i}}\bigr)\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U},i}}\Bigr).

Then
\hat{\rho}^{\circ}
is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable.
And

\bigl({({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}}\ \psi_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},i}}\bigr)\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U},i}=({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}({\psi_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},i}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U},i}})\in({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\cdot\mathsf{A}.

So
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\hat{\rho}^{\circ}\in({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\cdot\mathsf{A}
. Finally,

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\hat{\rho}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}}\Bigl({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\sum\nolimits_{i}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},i}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U},i}})}\Bigr)({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}}^{*}

\displaystyle=({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}}({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}})({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}}^{*}=({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}}\rho({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}}^{*}=\llbracket{{\underline{\mathbf{apply}}}\ U\ \underline{\mathbf{to}}\ \mathbf{X}}\rrbracket(\rho).

Hence
\llbracket{{\underline{\mathbf{apply}}}\ U\ \underline{\mathbf{to}}\ \mathbf{X}}\rrbracket(\rho)\vDash({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{X}})_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\cdot\mathsf{A}
.

\Box

Lemma 11

Rule
Init
is sound.

Proof.

\mathsf{A}

is a predicate on some variables
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}
.
We also have that
\mathbf{e}\notin\mathbf{E}

and that
\mathbf{e}

and
{\mathbf{x}}

have the same type because otherwise
\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{e}/{\mathbf{x}}}\}

would not be well-typed. Let

\mathsf{B}:=({\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{e}/{\mathbf{x}}}\},\ {{\mathbf{x}}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\lvert{0}\rangle}})
(the postcondition).
Note that there is an implicit conversion happening:
By definition,

\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{e}/{\mathbf{x}}}\}

is a predicate on
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{e}\mathbf{U}\setminus{\mathbf{x}}
,
so it does not make sense to intersect it (
\wedge
)
with
{\mathbf{x}}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\lvert{0}\rangle
.
But, as discussed at the end of
Section 2

(
\autopageref
page:pred.identify), we identify any predicate
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}

with
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}\otimes\ell^{2}[{{\mathbf{x}}}]
.
In particular,
\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{e}/{\mathbf{x}}}\}

is identified with
\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{e}/{\mathbf{x}}}\}\otimes\ell^{2}[{{\mathbf{x}}}]=:\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{e}/{\mathbf{x}}}\}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{e}\mathbf{U}}

on
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{e}\mathbf{U}
.
In that notation,
\mathsf{B}

is actually
\mathsf{B}=({\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{e}/{\mathbf{x}}}\}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{e}\mathbf{U}},\ {{\mathbf{x}}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\lvert{0}\rangle}})
,
a predicate over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{e}\mathbf{U}
.

To show the rule, we need to show that for any mixed memory

\rho

on
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}
,

\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}

implies
\rho\vDash\mathsf{B}
.

\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}

implies that there is a
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable

\rho^{\circ}

such that
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq\mathsf{A}

and
\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}=\rho
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be the canonical mapping from mixed memories over
{\mathbf{x}}

to mixed memories over
\mathbf{e}
. (Formally,

\mathcal{E}(\sigma)=U_{{\mathbf{x}}\to\mathbf{e}}\sigma U_{{\mathbf{x}}\to\mathbf{e}}^{*}

where
U_{{\mathbf{x}}\to\mathbf{e}}

was defined on
\autopageref
page:def:Urename.) Then

\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}\setminus{\mathbf{x}}}

maps mixed memories over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}

to mixed memories over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{e}\mathbf{U}\setminus{\mathbf{x}}

by renaming
{\mathbf{x}}
to
\mathbf{e}
. We define:

\hat{\rho}^{\circ}:=({\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}\setminus{\mathbf{x}}}})(\rho^{\circ})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}:=\hat{\rho}^{\circ}\otimes\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle_{{\mathbf{x}}}}).

Since
\rho^{\circ}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable,
and
{\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}\setminus{\mathbf{x}}}}

is the identity on
\mathbf{U}
, we have that
\hat{\rho}^{\circ}
is

(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{e}\setminus{\mathbf{x}},\mathbf{U})
-separable, and thus
\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}
is

(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{e},\mathbf{U})
-separable.

We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\hat{\rho}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}({U_{{\mathbf{x}}\to\mathbf{e}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}\setminus{\mathbf{x}}}})\rho^{\circ}({U_{{\mathbf{x}}\to\mathbf{e}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}\setminus{\mathbf{x}}}})^{*}

\displaystyle=({U_{{\mathbf{x}}\to\mathbf{e}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}\setminus{\mathbf{x}}}})\cdot\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq({U_{{\mathbf{x}}\to\mathbf{e}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}\setminus{\mathbf{x}}}})\cdot\mathsf{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\mathsf{A}\{{{\mathbf{x}}/\mathbf{e}}\}.

Here
(*)
is the definition of
\mathsf{A}\{{{\mathbf{x}}/\mathbf{e}}\}
(
\autopageref
page:def:psubst).
Thus

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}\subseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\hat{\rho}^{\circ}\otimes\ell^{2}[{{\mathbf{x}}}]\subseteq\mathsf{A}\{{{\mathbf{x}}/\mathbf{e}}\}\otimes\ell^{2}[{{\mathbf{x}}}]=\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{e}/{\mathbf{x}}}\}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{e}\mathbf{U}}.

And
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}\subseteq({\mathbf{x}}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\lvert{0}\rangle)
by definition of
\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}
.
Thus
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}\subseteq\mathsf{B}
.
Finally,

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{e}\mathbf{U}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}

\displaystyle=({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{e}\mathbf{U}}\hat{\rho}^{\circ}})\otimes\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle_{{\mathbf{x}}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}})\otimes\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle_{{\mathbf{x}}}})

\displaystyle=({\operatorname{tr}_{{\mathbf{x}}}{\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}}})\otimes\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle_{{\mathbf{x}}}})=({\operatorname{tr}_{{\mathbf{x}}}\rho})\otimes\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle_{{\mathbf{x}}}})=\llbracket{\underline{\mathbf{init}}\ {\mathbf{x}}}\rrbracket(\rho).

Here
(*)

uses that
\hat{\rho}^{\circ}

is the result of renaming
{\mathbf{x}}

to
\mathbf{e}

in
\rho^{\circ}
,
so tracing out
\mathbf{e}

in
\hat{\rho}^{\circ}

is the same as tracing out
{\mathbf{x}}
in
\rho^{\circ}
.

Altogether, we have
\llbracket{\underline{\mathbf{init}}\ {\mathbf{x}}}\rrbracket(\rho)\vDash\mathsf{B}
.

\Box

Lemma 12

Rule
If
is sound.

Proof.
The predicate
\mathsf{A}

is a space of quantum memories over some variables
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}
.
The predicate
\mathsf{B}
is a space of quantum memories over some variables

\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
.

We need to show that for any mixed memory
\rho

on
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}
,
if
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
,
then
\llbracket{\underline{\mathbf{if}}\ {\mathbf{x}}\ \underline{\mathbf{then}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\ \underline{\mathbf{else}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}}\rrbracket(\rho)\vDash\mathsf{B}
.
Recall that

\llbracket{\underline{\mathbf{if}}\ {\mathbf{x}}\ \underline{\mathbf{then}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\ \underline{\mathbf{else}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}}\rrbracket(\rho)=\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{1})+\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{0})
where

\rho_{i}:={\mathord{\downarrow_{i}}(\rho)}=({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{i}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\rho({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{i}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})^{*}
.

By
Lemma 9
, we have

\rho_{i}=({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{i}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\rho({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{i}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})^{*}\vDash({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{i}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\cdot\mathsf{A}
.
Since
\bigl\{{({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{1}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\cdot\mathsf{A}}\bigr\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\bigl\{{\mathsf{B}}\bigr\}

and
\bigl\{{({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\cdot\mathsf{A}}\bigr\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}\,}\bigl\{{\mathsf{B}}\bigr\}

by assumption of the
If
rule, it follows that

\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{1})\vDash\mathsf{B}

and
\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{0})\vDash\mathsf{B}
.
Thus with
Lemma 8
,

\llbracket{\underline{\mathbf{if}}\ {\mathbf{x}}\ \underline{\mathbf{then}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\ \underline{\mathbf{else}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}}\rrbracket(\rho)=\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{1})+\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{d}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{0})\vDash\mathsf{B}
.

\Box

Lemma 13

Rule
While
is sound.

Proof.
The predicate
\mathsf{A}

is a subspace of quantum memories over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}
for some
\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}
.

We need to show that for any mixed memory
\rho

on
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}
,
if
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
,
then
\llbracket{\underline{\mathbf{while}}\ {\mathbf{x}}\ \underline{\mathbf{do}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho)\vDash({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\cdot\mathsf{A}
.
Recall that

\llbracket{\underline{\mathbf{while}}\ {\mathbf{y}}\ \underline{\mathbf{do}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathord{\downarrow_{0}}(\rho_{n})

with
\rho_{n}:=\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket({\mathord{\downarrow_{1}}(\rho_{n-1})})

and
\rho_{0}:=\rho
,
where
\mathord{\downarrow_{1}},\mathord{\downarrow_{0}}

are defined by

\mathord{\downarrow_{i}}(\sigma):=({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{i}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\sigma({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{i}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})^{*}
.

We show
\rho_{n}\vDash\mathsf{A}

for
n\geq 0
by induction. The base case follows since
\rho_{0}=\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
.
For the induction step, assume that
\rho_{n}\vDash\mathsf{A}
.
Then
\mathord{\downarrow_{1}}(\rho_{n})=({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{1}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\rho_{n}({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{1}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})^{*}\vDash({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{1}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\cdot\mathsf{A}

by
Lemma 9
(
i
).
From the premise of the
While
rule, it then follows that

\rho_{n+1}=\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket\bigl({\mathord{\downarrow_{1}}(\rho_{n})}\bigr)\vDash\mathsf{A}
.

Since
\rho_{n}\vDash\mathsf{A}
,
we have
\mathord{\downarrow_{0}}(\rho_{n})\vDash({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\cdot\mathsf{A}

(again by
Lemma 9
(
i
)).
Since this holds for all
n
,
with
Lemma 8
we have that

\llbracket{\underline{\mathbf{while}}\ {\mathbf{y}}\ \underline{\mathbf{do}}\ \boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathord{\downarrow_{0}}(\rho_{n})\vDash({\mathsf{proj}({\lvert{0}\rangle})\,\mathbf{on}\,{\mathbf{x}}})\cdot\mathsf{A}
.

\Box

Lemma 14

Rule
Case
is sound.

Proof.
The predicate
\mathsf{A}

is a subspace of quantum memories over

\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}
for some variables
\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}

with
\mathbf{U}\subseteq\tilde{}\mathbf{U}
.
And
\mathsf{B}

is a subspace of quantum memories over

\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
for some variables
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
.

We need to show that if
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}

then
\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho)\vDash\mathsf{B}
.
Since
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
,
there is a
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},\tilde{}\mathbf{U})
-separable

\rho^{\circ}

with
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq\mathsf{A}

and
\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}=\rho
.
For making the notation more compact in the remainder of the proof,
let
\mathbf{V}:=\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{X}

and
\mathbf{W}:=\tilde{}\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{U}
.
In that notation,
\rho^{\circ}

is
(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V},\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W})
-separable.
Thus
\rho^{\circ}

can be written as

\rho^{\circ}=\sum_{i}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{\mathit{all},i}})

where
\psi_{\mathit{all},i}:=\psi_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V},i}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W},i}

for some quantum memories
\psi_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V},i}\neq 0

over
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V}
and
\psi_{\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W},i}\neq 0
over
\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}
.

Fix some
i
.
(We will omit
i

from the subscripts for now.) We can write
\psi_{\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}}\neq 0

as a nonempty sum

\psi_{\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}}=\sum_{j}\phi_{\mathbf{U},j}\otimes\phi_{\mathbf{W},j}

with quantum memories
\phi_{\mathbf{U},j},\phi_{\mathbf{W},j}\neq 0

over
\mathbf{U}

and
\mathbf{W}

respectively where the
\phi_{\mathbf{W},j}
are orthogonal.
We have
\psi_{\mathit{all}}\in\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq\mathsf{A}\subseteq\mathsf{C}
.
Thus

\psi_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V}}\otimes\phi_{\mathbf{U},j}\otimes\phi_{\mathbf{W},j}=({\mathsf{proj}({\phi_{\mathbf{W},j}})\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{W}})\,\psi_{\mathit{all}}\in({\mathsf{proj}({\phi_{\mathbf{W},j}})\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{W}})\cdot\mathsf{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{\subseteq}}\mathsf{C}.

Here
(*)
follows from
\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{C})\cap\mathbf{W}=\varnothing
.
Hence
\psi_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V}}\otimes\phi_{\mathbf{U},j}\in\mathsf{C}
for all
j
.
(Note here that the notation
\mathsf{C}

is overloaded both as a predicate over
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}

and over
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{U}
, see
\autopageref
page:pred.identify.)
Thus
\psi_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V}}\otimes\phi_{\mathbf{U},j^{*}}\in\mathsf{C}
for some
j^{*}
.
(Recall that the sum
\psi_{\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}}=\sum_{j}\phi_{\mathbf{U},j}\otimes\phi_{\mathbf{W},j}
was nonempty.)

Since
\mathsf{C}

is
M
-disentangling,
this implies that
\psi_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V}}=\psi_{\mathbf{X}}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{V}}

for some
\psi_{\mathbf{V}}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{V}}]

and
\psi_{\mathbf{X}}\in M
.
Thus

\psi_{\mathit{all}}=\psi_{\mathbf{X}}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{V}}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}}\in(\mathbf{X}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi_{\mathbf{X}})
.
Since also
\psi_{\mathit{all}}\in\mathsf{A}
,
we have that

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{\mathit{all}}})\subseteq({\mathbf{X}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi_{\mathbf{X}},\ \mathsf{A}})
.
And since
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{V}=\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}

and
\mathbf{U}\mathbf{W}=\tilde{}\mathbf{U}
,

\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{\mathit{all}}})
is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},\tilde{}\mathbf{U})
-separable. Hence

\rho_{\mathit{all}}:=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{\mathit{all}}})\vDash(\mathbf{X}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi_{\mathbf{X}})\land\mathsf{A}
.
By assumption of the rule
Case
,

\{{\mathbf{X}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi_{\mathbf{X}},\ \mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}
(since
\psi_{\mathbf{X}}\in M
). Thus
\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{\mathit{all}})\vDash\mathsf{B}
.

We now “unfix”
i
. We thus have
\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{\mathit{all},i})\vDash\mathsf{B}
for

\rho_{\mathit{all},i}:=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{\mathit{all},i}})
. Furthermore,

\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho)=\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}})=\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket\Bigl({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}}\sum\nolimits_{i}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{\mathit{all},i}})}\Bigr)=\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket\Bigl({\sum\nolimits_{i}\rho_{\mathit{all},i}}\Bigr)=\sum\nolimits_{i}\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket({\rho_{\mathit{all},i}}).

By
Lemma 8
,
\forall i.\,\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{\mathit{all},i})\vDash\mathsf{B}
implies

\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho)=\sum\nolimits_{i}\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket({\rho_{\mathit{all},i}})\vDash\mathsf{B}
.

\Box

The following is an auxiliary lemma needed for the proof of

rule
Universe
. But it is also of independent interest because it
says that the choice of the set of program variables with respect to
which we evaluate a program (denoted
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}

on
\autopageref
page:XXall) does not matter as long as it is large enough.

Lemma 15
(Changing the set of program variables)

Let
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}^{\mathbf{X}}

denote Hoare judgments
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}
as in
Definition 2
, except that the
set
\mathbf{X}
is used instead of
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}
.
(I.e., the semantics of the program
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}

are defined with respect to memories containing variables
\mathbf{X}
,
not
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}
.)

Assume that
\mathsf{fv}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}),\mathsf{progvars}(\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{A})),\mathsf{progvars}(\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{B}))\subseteq\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{2}
.
Let
\mathbf{Y}_{i}:=\mathbf{X}_{i}\setminus\mathsf{fv}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}})\setminus\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{A})\setminus\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{B})
.
Let
T_{i}

be the type of
\mathbf{Y}_{i}
.
Assume that
\lvert{T_{1}}\rvert\geq\lvert{T_{2}}\rvert

or
{T_{1}}

is infinite. Then

\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}^{\mathbf{X}_{1}}\implies\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}^{\mathbf{X}_{2}}
.

Proof.
We fix some
\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U}

such that
\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}

contains all ghosts from
\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{A}),\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{B})
.
By
Lemma 1
, we can interpret
\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}

in judgments
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A},\rho\vDash\mathsf{B}

as predicates over
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}

(where
\rho

is over
\mathbf{X}
),
i.e., we can without loss of generality use the same
\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}

everywhere.
Note that
\mathbf{X}_{1}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{1}=\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}
since
both are equal to
\mathsf{fv}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}})\cup\mathsf{progvars}(\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{A}))\cup\mathsf{progvars}(\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{B}))
.

Assume
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}^{\mathbf{X}_{1}}
.
To show
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}^{\mathbf{X}_{2}}
,
we fix a mixed memory
\rho

over
\mathbf{X}_{2}

with
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
,
and we need to show
\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho)\vDash\mathsf{B}
.

Let

S:=\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}\rho\subseteq\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{Y}_{2}}]
.
The operator
\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}\rho

can be written as
\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{i}})

with some orthonormal
\psi_{i}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{Y}_{2}}]

and some
p_{i}>0

with

\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}=\operatorname{tr}({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}\rho})<\infty
. Thus
I

is countable (otherwise
\sum_{i\in I}p_{i}

cannot converge). Hence
\dim S=\dim\operatorname{span}\{\psi_{i}\}_{i\in I}=\lvert{I}\rvert

is countable. Furthermore

\dim S\leq\dim\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{Y}_{2}}]=\lvert{T_{2}}\rvert
.
Since
\lvert{T_{1}}\rvert\geq\lvert{T_{2}}\rvert

or
\lvert{T_{1}}\rvert

is infinite, it follows that

\dim S\leq\lvert{T_{1}}\rvert=\dim\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{Y}_{1}}]
.
Thus there exists an isometry
U

from
S

to
\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{Y}_{1}}]
.
We extend
U

to an operator from
\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{Y}_{2}}]

to
\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{Y}_{1}}]

by setting
U=0

on the orthogonal complement of
S
.
Then
U^{*}U

is the projector
P_{S}

onto
S
.
Since
S=\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}\rho
,

S\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}]\supseteq\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho
.
And
{({P_{S}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}_{2}})}

is the projector onto
S\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}]
. Hence
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho

is fixed by
{({P_{S}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}_{2}})}
.

Let
\mathcal{E}(\sigma):=U\sigma U^{*}

for all
\sigma

over
\mathbf{Y}_{2}
.
And let

\hat{\mathcal{E}}(\sigma):=({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}_{2}})\sigma({U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}_{2}})^{*}
for all
\sigma

over
\mathbf{X}_{2}
,
i.e.,

\hat{\mathcal{E}}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}\otimes{\mathcal{E}}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}^{*}(\sigma):=U^{*}\sigma U

for all
\sigma

over
\mathbf{Y}_{1}
.
And let

\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{*}(\sigma):=({U^{*}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}_{1}})\sigma({U^{*}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}_{1}})^{*}
for all
\sigma

over
\mathbf{X}_{1}
,
i.e.,

\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{*}=\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}_{1}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{E}^{*}
.
We have

\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{*}\circ\hat{\mathcal{E}}(\rho)=({U^{*}U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}_{2}})\rho({U^{*}U\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}_{2}})^{*}=({P_{S}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}_{2}})\rho({P_{S}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}_{2}})^{*}=\rho
.
Here the last equality is
because
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho
is fixed by
({P_{S}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{Y}_{2}})
.

Since
\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{A})\cap\mathbf{Y}_{2}=\varnothing
and
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
,
by
Lemma 9
(
ii
),

\hat{\mathcal{E}}(\rho)\vDash\mathsf{A}
.
Note that
{\hat{\mathcal{E}}(\rho)}

is a mixed memory over
\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{Y}_{1}=\mathbf{X}_{1}
.
Since
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}^{\mathbf{X}_{1}}
,
we have

\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{1}}\bigl({\hat{\mathcal{E}}(\rho)}\bigr)\vDash\mathsf{B}
.
Here
\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{1}}

denotes the semantics
\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket
of
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}

defined with respect to the set of variables
\mathbf{X}_{1}

instead of
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}
. Note that

\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{1}}=\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{1}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{1}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}_{1}}

and

\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{2}}=\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}_{2}}
(since
\mathsf{fv}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}})\cap\mathbf{Y}_{1}=\mathsf{fv}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}})\cap\mathbf{Y}_{2}=\varnothing
).
Then

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{*}\Bigl({\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{1}}\bigl({\hat{\mathcal{E}}(\rho)}\bigr)}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=({\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}_{1}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{1}}\otimes\mathcal{E}^{*}})\circ({\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{1}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{1}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}_{1}}})\circ({\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}\otimes\mathcal{E}})(\rho)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\Bigl({\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}\otimes({\mathcal{E}^{*}\circ\mathcal{E}})}\Bigr)(\rho)=\bigl({\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}_{2}}}\bigr)\circ\bigl({\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}\otimes({\mathcal{E}^{*}\circ\mathcal{E}})}\bigr)(\rho)

\displaystyle=\bigl({\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}_{2}}}\bigr)\circ({\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{*}\circ\hat{\mathcal{E}}})(\rho)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\Bigl({\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}}\otimes\mathsf{id}_{\mathbf{Y}_{2}}}\Bigr)(\rho)=\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{2}}(\rho).

(2)

In
(*)
we use that
\mathbf{X}_{1}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{1}=\mathbf{X}_{2}\setminus\mathbf{Y}_{2}
.
And
(**)
follows since
\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{*}\circ\hat{\mathcal{E}}(\rho)=\rho
(shown above).

Since
\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{B})\cap\mathbf{Y}_{1}=\varnothing

and
\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{1}}\bigl({\hat{\mathcal{E}}(\rho)}\bigr)\vDash\mathsf{B}
,
by
Lemma 9
(
ii
),

\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{*}\bigl({{\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{1}}({\hat{\mathcal{E}}(\rho)})}}\bigr)\vDash\mathsf{B}
. By (
2
), this implies

\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket^{\mathbf{X}_{2}}(\rho)\vDash\mathsf{B}
.
Since
\rho

was an arbitrary mixed memory over
\mathbf{X}_{2}

with
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
, this implies
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}^{\mathbf{X}_{2}}
.

\Box

Lemma 16

Rule
Universe
is sound.

Most of the work for the proof has already been done in

Lemma 15
.

Proof.
By
Lemma 15
(with
\mathbf{X}_{1}:=\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}
and
\mathbf{X}_{2}:=\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}
), we have that
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}^{\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}}

(in the notation of
Lemma 15
) implies
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}
.
(For this, note that
\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\setminus\mathsf{fv}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}})\setminus\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{A})\setminus\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{B})

contains
{\mathbf{x}}

and thus has infinite type.)
Thus to prove the soundness of
Universe
, it is sufficient to prove

\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}^{\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}}
.

To show
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}^{\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}}
,
fix some
\rho

over
\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}

with
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
.
Then there is a
(\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable

\rho^{\circ}
over
\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}

with
\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}=\rho

and
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq\mathsf{A}
.

Since
\rho^{\circ}

is
(\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable,
we can write
\rho^{\circ}=\sum_{i}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{i}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\prime}})

with
\psi_{i}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{E}}]

and
\psi_{i}^{\prime}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{U}}]
and
\psi_{i},\psi_{i}^{\prime}\neq 0
.
Since
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq\mathsf{A}
,
we have
\psi_{i}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{A}

for all
i
.
Furthermore, we have

\psi_{i}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\prime}\in({\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi_{i}})

and
\psi_{i}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\prime}\in({\mathbf{U}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi_{i}^{\prime}})
.
Thus

\psi_{i}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{A}_{i}:=({\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi_{i},\ \mathbf{U}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi_{i}^{\prime},\ \mathsf{A}})
. We then have that

\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{i}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\prime}})

is
(\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable
and
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{i}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\prime}})\subseteq\mathsf{A}_{i}
.
Thus
\rho_{i}:=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{i}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\prime}})\vDash\mathsf{A}_{i}
.
By assumption of the
rule
Universe
we have

\{{\mathsf{A}_{i}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}
.
By
Lemma 15
(with
\mathbf{X}_{1}:=\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}
and
\mathbf{X}_{2}:=\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}
),

\{{\mathsf{A}_{i}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}^{\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}}
. (For this, note that
\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\subseteq\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}
since otherwise

\{{\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathbf{U}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi^{\prime},\ \mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}
in the rule’s premise would not be well-defined. Hence
the cardinality of the type of
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\setminus\mathsf{fv}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}},\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})
is at least as big as that of
\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}\setminus\mathsf{fv}(\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}},\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B})
.)
Since
\rho_{i}\vDash\mathsf{A}_{i}
, we have
\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{i})\vDash\mathsf{B}
. This holds for all
i
.

We have

\rho=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}=\sum_{i}\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{i}\otimes\psi_{i}^{\prime}})=\sum_{i}\rho_{i}
.
Thus
\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho)=\sum_{i}\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{i})
.
Since for all
i
,

\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{i})\vDash\mathsf{B}
,
by
Lemma 8
we have

\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho)=\sum_{i}\llbracket{\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}}\rrbracket(\rho_{i})\vDash\mathsf{B}
.
Since this holds for all
\rho
over
\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}

with
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
,
we have shown
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}^{\mathbf{X}{\mathbf{x}}}
.
As mentioned in the beginning of the proof, by

Lemma 15
this implies
\{{\mathsf{A}}\}{\,\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{c}}\,}\{{\mathsf{B}}\}
.

\Box

Lemma 17

Rule
Transmute
is sound.

Proof.
We distinguish four cases:
\mathbf{G},\mathbf{G}^{\prime}

are entangled ghosts (EE-case),
\mathbf{G},\mathbf{G}^{\prime}

are unentangled ghosts (UU-case),
\mathbf{G}

are entangled and
\mathbf{G}^{\prime}

are unentangled ghosts (EU-case), and
\mathbf{G}

are unentangled and
\mathbf{G}^{\prime}

are entangled ghosts (UE-case). Most of the proof is the same in all
four cases, we make case distinctions in individual proof steps as
necessary.

\mathsf{A}

is a predicate over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}

for some
\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U}
.
And
\mathsf{A}^{\prime}:=\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{G}/\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}\}

is a predicate over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}

for some
\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
.
(What variables are in
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}

and
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
, respectively, depends on the case. E.g., in the EU-case,

\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}
and
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}=\mathbf{U}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}
.)

To show

\mathsf{A}\Rrightarrow\bigvee\nolimits_{\!i}\bigl({({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})\cdot\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{G}^{\prime}/\mathbf{G}}\}}\bigr)=:\mathsf{B}
, we fix
some mixed memory
\rho

over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}
with
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
. We have to show that
\rho\vDash\mathsf{B}
.

Since
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
,
there exists a
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable

\rho^{\circ}

over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}

with
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq\mathsf{A}

and
\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}=\rho
.

Let

\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}:=U_{\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}\rho^{\circ}U_{\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}^{*}
.
We have that

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}=U_{\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}\cdot\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq U_{\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}\cdot\mathsf{A}=\mathsf{A}\{{\mathbf{G}^{\prime}/\mathbf{G}}\}=\mathsf{A}^{\prime}
.

In the EE- and EU-case,
\mathbf{G}\subseteq\mathbf{E}
.
That is,
U_{\mathbf{G}\to\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}

renames only variables in
\mathbf{E}
.
Then since
\rho^{\circ}
is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U})
-separable,

\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}
is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime},\mathbf{U})
-separable
in that case. Similarly, in the UE- and UU-case,
\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}

is then
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime})
-separable.

And since
\mathbf{G}\cap\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}=\varnothing
, we have
\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}=\rho
.

Note that this does
not
imply that
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}^{\prime}

because
\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}

is not necessarily
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\mathbf{U}^{\prime})
-separable.
(The separability is along the wrong variables, at least in the EU-
and UE-cases.)

Let

\rho^{\circ}_{i}:=({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})^{*}
and
\rho_{i}:=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}}\rho^{\circ}_{i}
.
Then

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}_{i}=({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})\cdot\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}\subseteq({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})\cdot\mathsf{A}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{B}
.

We show that
\rho^{\circ}_{i}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\mathbf{U}^{\prime})
-separable
by distinguishing four cases:
In the EE-case,

\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime},\mathbf{U})
-separable.
And
({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})

operates only on
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime}
,
hence
\rho^{\circ}_{i}

is also
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime},\mathbf{U})
-separable.
And
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime}
,

\mathbf{U}^{\prime}=\mathbf{U}
,
hence
\rho^{\circ}_{i}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\mathbf{U}^{\prime})
-separable.

In the UU-case,

\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime})
-separable.
And
({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})

operates only on
\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime}
,
hence
\rho^{\circ}_{i}

is also
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime})
-separable.
And
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=\mathbf{E}
,

\mathbf{U}^{\prime}=\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime}
,
hence
\rho^{\circ}_{i}
is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\mathbf{U}^{\prime})
-separable.

In the EU-case,

\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime},\mathbf{U})
-separable.
Thus we can write
\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}

as

\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}=\sum_{j}\mathsf{proj}({\psi_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U}}})
for some

\psi_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}]

and
\psi_{\mathbf{U}}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{U}}]
.
Then

\rho^{\circ}_{i}=\sum_{j}\mathsf{proj}({\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime},j}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U},j}})

with

\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime},j}:=({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})\psi_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime},j}
.
Since
M_{i}

has rank
\leq 1
,

\operatorname{im}M_{i}=\operatorname{span}\{\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}\}

for some
\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}]

(in the case
\operatorname{rank}M_{i}=0
,
we simply use
\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}=0
).
Thus

\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{G}^{\prime},j}=\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G},j}\otimes\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}

for some

\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G},j}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}}]
.
Hence

\rho^{\circ}_{i}=\sum_{j}\mathsf{proj}\bigl({\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G},j}\otimes(\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}\otimes\psi_{\mathbf{U},j})}\bigr)
, thus
\rho^{\circ}_{i}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G},\mathbf{U}\mathbf{G}^{\prime})
-separable.
Since in the EU-case,
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{G}

and
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{G}^{\prime}
,
we have that
\rho^{\circ}_{i}
is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\mathbf{U}^{\prime})
-separable.

In the UE-case,
we show that

\rho^{\circ}_{i}=\sum_{j}\mathsf{proj}\bigl({(\psi_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},j}\otimes\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})\otimes\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{G},j}}\bigr)
for some

\psi_{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E},j}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}}]
,

\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{G}^{\prime}}]
,
and
\psi^{\prime}_{\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{G},j}\in\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{G},j}]
(analogous to the EU-case).
Thus
\rho^{\circ}_{i}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{G}^{\prime},\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{G})
-separable.
Since in the UE-case,
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=\mathbf{E}\mathbf{G}^{\prime}

and
\mathbf{U}^{\prime}=\mathbf{U}\setminus\mathbf{G}
,
we have that
\rho^{\circ}_{i}
is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\mathbf{U}^{\prime})
-separable.

Thus in all four cases,
\rho^{\circ}_{i}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\mathbf{U}^{\prime})
-separable.
Furthermore, we showed above that
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}_{i}\subseteq\mathsf{B}
,
and defined
\rho_{i}:=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}}\rho^{\circ}_{i}
. Thus
\rho_{i}\vDash\mathsf{B}
.

Let

\mathcal{E}(\sigma):=\sum_{i}({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})\sigma({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})^{*}
. Since
\sum_{i}M_{i}^{*}M_{i}=\mathsf{id}
,

\mathcal{E}

is trace-preserving.

\sum_{i}\rho_{i}=\sum_{i}\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}}\rho^{\circ}_{i}=\sum_{i}\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}}({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}({M_{i}\,\mathbf{on}\,\mathbf{G}^{\prime}})^{*}=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}}\mathcal{E}(\tilde{\rho}^{\circ})
. Since
\mathcal{E}

is trace-preserving and operates only on
\mathbf{G}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}
,
we have

\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}}\mathcal{E}(\tilde{\rho}^{\circ})=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\mathbf{U}^{\prime}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}=\rho
. Thus

\sum_{i}\rho_{i}=\rho
.
By
Lemma 8
,
\rho_{i}\vDash\mathsf{B}

then implies
\rho\vDash\mathsf{B}
.

Since this holds for all
\rho\vDash\mathsf{A}
, we have
\mathsf{A}\Rrightarrow\mathsf{B}
.

\Box

Lemma 18

Rule
ShapeShift
is sound.

Proof.
Since
(\mathbf{E}\cup\mathbf{E}^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{fv}(\mathsf{A})=\varnothing
,
we can interpret
\mathsf{A}
as a predicate
over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}

for some
\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}
with
\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\cap(\mathbf{E}\cup\mathbf{E}^{\prime})=\varnothing
. (See the discussion
on
\autopageref
page:pred.identify about identifying predicates over different sets.)
Then the pre- and postconditions

{({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}})}
and

{({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi^{\prime},\ \mathsf{A}})}
can be written more explicitly as predicates

{({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}}]})}
and

{({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi^{\prime},\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}]})}

over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}
and
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}
, respectively. (Justified by

Lemma 1
.)
And
\psi,\psi^{\prime}
are quantum memories over
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}

and
\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}
, respectively.

Fix a mixed memory
\rho

over
\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}

with
\rho\vDash({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}}]})
.
To show
rule
ShapeShift
, we need to show that

\rho\vDash({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi^{\prime},\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}]})
.

Since

\rho\vDash({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}}]})
,
there is an
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E},\tilde{}\mathbf{U})
-separable

\rho^{\circ}

with

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq{({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}}]})}
and
\operatorname{tr}_{\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}=\rho
.

Thus
\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq{({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi})}
.
Hence
\rho^{\circ}=({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}}\rho^{\circ}})\otimes\mathsf{proj}({\psi})
.
Let

\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}:=({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}}\rho^{\circ}})\otimes\mathsf{proj}({\psi^{\prime}})
.
Then

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}}\rho^{\circ}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}}\bigl({({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}}\rho^{\circ}})\otimes\mathsf{proj}({\psi})}\bigr)=({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}}\rho^{\circ}})\otimes\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}}\rho^{\circ}})\otimes\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\mathsf{proj}({\psi^{\prime}})=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\bigl({({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}}\rho^{\circ}})\otimes\mathsf{proj}({\psi})}\bigr)=\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}.

(3)

Here
(*)
is by assumption from
rule
ShapeShift
.
Then

\operatorname{tr}_{\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}=\operatorname{tr}_{\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}}\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{eq:rel-rho-tilderho}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\operatorname{tr}_{\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}}\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}}\rho^{\circ}=\operatorname{tr}_{\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}}\rho^{\circ}=\rho.

Since
\rho^{\circ}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E},\tilde{}\mathbf{U})
-separable,

\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}}\rho^{\circ}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{X},\tilde{}\mathbf{U})
-separable,
and hence

\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}=({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}}\rho^{\circ}})\otimes\mathsf{proj}({\psi^{\prime}})
is

(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\setminus\mathbf{X}\mathbin{\mathaccent 0{\cdot}\cup}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\tilde{}\mathbf{U})=(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\tilde{}\mathbf{U})
-separable.

Since

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq{({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}}]})}
, we have

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\rho^{\circ}\subseteq{{\mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}}]}}
.
By
Lemma 7
, this implies

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}}{\rho^{\circ}}\subseteq{{\mathsf{A}}}
.
Then by (
3
),

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}{\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}}\subseteq{{\mathsf{A}}}
.
And by
Lemma 7
,

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}{\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}}\subseteq{{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}]}
.
And since

\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}=({\operatorname{tr}_{\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}}\rho^{\circ}})\otimes\mathsf{proj}({\psi^{\prime}})
, we also have

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}\subseteq({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=\psi^{\prime}})
.
Thus

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}\subseteq{({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi^{\prime},\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}]})}
.

Since
\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}

is
(\mathbf{X}^{\mathbf{all}}\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\tilde{}\mathbf{U})
-separable
and satisfies

\operatorname{\mathbf{supp}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}\subseteq{({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi^{\prime},\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}]})}
and

\operatorname{tr}_{\tilde{}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\tilde{}\mathbf{U}}\tilde{\rho}^{\circ}=\rho
, it follows that

\rho\vDash({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi^{\prime},\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}]})
.

Since
\rho

was arbitrary with

\rho\vDash({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}}]})
,
we have shown

{({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi,\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}}]})}\Rrightarrow{({\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=_{\mathfrak{q}}\psi^{\prime},\ \mathsf{A}\otimes\ell^{2}[{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}]})}
, the
conclusion of the rule.

\Box

7
Derived rules

In this section, we show that our eleven core rules are powerful enough
to derive a number of new rules without having to refer to the
semantics of Hoare judgments with ghosts from

Definition 2
. (That is, the rules in this section would hold
for any definition of Hoare judgments satisfying the eleven core rules.)

This first derived rule is relatively trivial but of high importance:
